INV-200 | **A colour a `<canvas>` reads through `getPropertyValue('--t') || '#hex'` is a second source of truth, and the fallback must EQUAL the token's Console-light value; a hex literal equal to a declared token value is banned everywhere else outside a named INV-166 freeze (proposed by the cycle-19 reflection, written in Batch P 2026-09-11).** A canvas cannot read `var(--x)` at paint time, so the QA waveform and the EmpDocs signature pad read the token first and fall back to a literal on a page with no stylesheet — and writing the scan found all THREE stale (`--accent` falling back to `--accent-2`'s value; two values no token declares). The reflection's proposal also carried a two-sided per-file RATCHET over every other chromatic literal; Batch P RETIRED that half before recording the invariant — it was a hand-reasoned baseline to maintain on every colour edit, guarding literals that fire on no real page, and its one recorded token candidate (the clock ribbon's Console-only rgba fallback twins) rides `--accent-glow` / `--warn-glow` now, the color-mix line still setting the strength so the render is byte-identical. The rule that survives is the F7 shape: ONE home per colour. Verify: TW-B (derived token set, the canvas regex incl. the `(… || '').trim()` shape, the FROZEN exactly-once-inside-its-selector — a re-added token-equal literal and a moved canvas fallback both bite) + P2 (the three ribbon fallbacks on the glow tokens; `--warn-glow` in exactly the two base blocks and in no palette block; no rgba twin left in the partial; pixel-compared) | Subsystem: Client (shell) + Client (Time Clock views) + Test Suite
INV-201 | **Every JSON-blob Script Property is written through `propSetBounded_` — operator blobs REFUSE by name with nothing written, auto-managed blobs DEGRADE through a named shrinker, and a scalar writer is allowlisted BY NAME (Batch Q, 2026-09-11).** Script Properties cap at ~9KB per value and 500KB per store, while the endpoints' own entry caps admit far more (50 email templates × 4000 chars is ~200KB into a ~9KB slot) — and because every save endpoint writes its `AdminConfigChange` audit row before returning, a `setProperty` throw produced an opaque platform error beside an audit row claiming the change landed. Size is measured in BYTES by `utf8Len_`, never `.length` (UTF-16 units under-count anything non-ASCII; a surrogate pair is 4 bytes). The MODE is the decision: a blob an operator edits REFUSES with an error naming the key, the size, the cap and what to shorten — nothing is written, and each of the 14 call sites sits inside the endpoint try whose catch already returns `{success:false, error}` to its editor, so the refusal reaches the person who can act on it. A blob nobody is watching (the 6 heartbeat/cache/stamp maps) DEGRADES instead, because refusing would simply lose the write: `propShrinkDropOldest_` drops oldest-first UNTIL IT FITS (not one per call — a caller's retry loop is a safety net, and a large map would exhaust it and clear the property), `propShrinkStripFields_` costs the self-test result its free text before its figures, and two custom shrinkers reset a cache on BYTES. An UNSHRINKABLE degrade DELETES the property and LOGS why rather than leaving a stale value — every degrade target regenerates, and "no heartbeat recorded yet" is honest where a frozen stamp is not. The 9 scalar writers (a day count, a dollar cap, a model key, a folder id, a generation counter) stay on `setProperty` and are allowlisted by name with a reason each. Companion rule: the SERIALIZED size is shown to the operator by one shared badge (`propBudgetHtml_`, the KDD above), and `scriptPropertiesStatus_` reports the store's usage on Admin → System — read-only, values COUNTED never returned, `bytes: null` on a failed read (INV-187), an ok FACT while comfortable (INV-186). Verify: Q-1 (DERIVED — every `setProperty` outside the helper writes an allowlisted scalar, every blob key IS bounded, and the refuse/degrade mode is asserted per key, so a new blob property fails CI until it picks one), Q-1b (the helper and both shrinkers driven in a vm: refuse writes NOTHING and names key/size/cap, degrade keeps the NEWEST entry, an unshrinkable value deletes rather than leaving a stale one, `utf8Len_` counts bytes), Q-2 (every save endpoint, the one-home badge rule, and the WORST-CASE arithmetic so a raised entry cap reads as the over-budget it is), Q-3, plus a `saveEmailTemplates` over-size case in the editor suite | Subsystem: Server + Client (shell) + Client (Call Notes views) + Client (Reference views)
INV-202 | **A derived count that reads a source file BY PATH is coupled to that file's layout, and a batch that moves, renames or splits that file must repoint every derivation in the SAME commit (proposed by the second cycle-19 reflection 2026-09-14, written in Batch D2).** `scripts/counts.mjs` reads `web-app/Code.js`, `web-app/Tests.js`, `test/visual/shoot.mjs` and the Invariant Library by path; the coupling is INVISIBLE until the move, so it is discovered by a red `--check` rather than by reading either file. It fails CLOSED — a merge where the document and the tree disagree is refused — which is the correct direction but makes it a MERGE BLOCKER, not a warning, so the repoint belongs in the moving commit and not in a follow-up. The derivations are ENUMERABLE, which is what makes the repoint mechanical rather than remembered: `grep -n "read('" scripts/counts.mjs` lists every path the counts depend on. Batch D1 is the first application (the Invariant Library moved to `.cycle/config.md` and `libraryFile()` moved with it, with a CLAUDE.md fallback so the count survives either layout); Batch F1 discharged the obligation ahead of the split — `serverSource()`, derived from `filePushOrder`, is the ONE way a pin or a count reads the server, and Batch F2 then split `Code.js` into fourteen files with every derived figure unchanged, which is the acceptance test this entry named. | Test Suite + Server | Verify: F1's `serverSource()` pin must assert `counts.mjs` resolves through it, not only `extractRawFunction`; `node scripts/counts.mjs --check` green on the post-F2 tree is the acceptance test — PASSED 2026-09-14, with all eleven figures identical across the split.
INV-203 | **An obligation that is REMEMBERED drifts; an obligation that is DERIVED cannot (proposed by the second cycle-19 reflection 2026-09-14, written in Batch D2).** A process rule whose only enforcement is a printed reminder has no enforcement — record the datum at the moment it is known, in a file a later step READS, or expect it to be missing. Evidence: Batch P1 added a SessionStart reminder, a STATE.md `Estimates:` line and a required `Estimate:`/`Actual:` pair per implement block; two batches later Batch C's own block shipped without those lines, and the 2026-09-14 reflection reconstructed all four figures from STATE.md prose — the sixth consecutive reflection to do so. The generalization is Batch C's move applied beyond numbers: the cure for a hand-carried fact is to derive it from the artefact that defines it, and the cure for a hand-carried PROCESS is a check that reads the artefact the process is supposed to produce. | Test Suite + .cycle | Verify: a `/reflect`-time scan asserting every `*-broad-implement.md` written after the convention landed carries both an `Estimate:` and an `Actual:` line — NOT YET BUILT (the honest boundary is the hard part: block filenames do not sort chronologically and a CI checkout has no useful mtimes, so the scan needs a declared grandfather set, the Doc-map-declaration shape D1 used for the append-only archives).
INV-204 | **A DRY-RUN preview of a mutating job must go through the job's OWN resolver and write nothing, and both halves are pinned rather than intended (operator 2026-09-14, `previewPtoAccruals`).** A preview exists to be ACTED ON — an operator reads it and decides whether to let the job run, or hand-edits a stamp because of what it said — so the two claims it makes implicitly are the two that must be checked. (a) SAME RESOLVER: the decision half of `creditMonthlyPtoAccruals` was extracted into `planPtoAccrualRun_` and is called, not copied; a preview that re-derives the plan reassures about arithmetic nobody runs and drifts on the first policy change (the g59 shape — a DRAFT preview goes through the ONE resolver — and g45's duplicated-layout class). (b) WRITES NOTHING: no `setValue` / `appendRow` / `deleteRow`, no balance mutator, no audit row, in the preview OR the resolver — asserted on both bodies, because “nothing was written” is a claim with consequences and a comment is not a check. Two deliberate asymmetries with the job, both of which a future preview should copy: it takes NO ScriptLock (a diagnostic must not queue behind live traffic; the price is that a mid-run preview reads half-applied state, so the job stays the record), and it REPORTS the feature flag the job short-circuits on, because an operator asking why nothing happened is better served by “here is the switch” than by an empty report. | Server + Test Suite | Verify: the `previewPtoAccruals` Node pin (one `planPtoAccrualRun_(` call in the preview, zero re-derivation of the plan, the three write shapes absent from both bodies, no `LockService`, the manager gate, the flag reported) + the editor-suite `previewPtoAccrual_predictsTheCredit` (the preview's predicted days equal what the credit then lands, and neither the balance nor the stamp moved while previewing). EXTENDED 2026-09-15 by inspect mode, which is the same rule on a DIAGNOSTIC that deliberately ignores state the real job obeys: it must SAY it is ignoring it (the header line, the per-rep SETTLED marker with its remedy, a total phrased as a valuation rather than a pending credit), and its input guards must be PURE so a pin can run them — written inline first, the pin asserted only that the error message appeared in the source and a bite-check deleting the `if` around it left the harness green.
INV-205 | **A job that CLOSES a period must RECONCILE it afterwards, and the reconciliation is UPWARD-ONLY, FAILS CLOSED, and reads a ledger that IS the record (operator 2026-09-15, `creditMonthlyPtoAccruals`).** An idempotency key of “this period was processed” is safe only while the period's inputs are frozen. The Timesheet is not frozen on the 1st — a missing-punch approval, a manager day edit and a direct Sheet edit all land later — so the column-R stamp made each month's valuation permanent at the instant of the first run, and all three PH reps were credited ZERO for 2026-08 on days closed by approvals days afterwards, with no error and no retry. The key must instead be the VALUE already committed (here: hours paid for), so a later change is a difference to settle. Three properties, each of which generalises: (a) UPWARD ONLY — fewer readable hours than were credited is a reported SHORTFALL, never a claw-back, because a script does not quietly take back something a person has been told they have; (b) FAIL CLOSED — a period outside the window, hours that will not compute, or a ledger read that never reached past the window are reported and skipped, because “cannot read what was committed” must never collapse into “nothing was committed” and commit it all again; (c) the LEDGER IS THE RECORD — the `PtoAccrualCredit` audit rows, not a second store that can disagree with them, which makes the note format a CONTRACT (writer and parser are a pinned mirror pair) and the row load-bearing (all three accrual writes are `writeWitnessAuditLog_`, since a dropped row reads as “never credited”). Cost is bounded by construction: the window rides the resolver's existing range rather than opening a second full read inside the ScriptLock (C17-9), and the ledger reads a bounded tail of the AuditLog and REPORTS a scan that could not see the whole window. | Server + Test Suite | Verify: the R pin (round-trip mirror incl. the fail-closed nulls; the window incl. the year boundary and the current-month exclusion; all four verdicts driven — topup, ok, shortfall, skipped; only a topup reaches `adjustLeaveBalance_`; one range build; the bounded ledger read and its truncated flag) + the editor suite's `accrualReconcile_topsUpLateData`, which replays the 2026-08 sequence end to end: open day credits zero, approval lands late, the next run tops up, a re-run does not, a deleted punch reports instead of clawing back.
INV-206 | **A recovery is not a prevention: a job that HEALS late data owes a separate signal for what made it late, and that signal must report only what can still be acted on (operator 2026-09-15, `checkOpenPunches`).** INV-205 made late punch data heal itself; nothing stopped a day sitting clock-in-only for five days, and the in-progress month had NO open-punch visibility at all because the accrual only ever looked at months it owed. `checkOpenPunches` walks the WHOLE roster daily — an open day breaks the ADP export, the pay statement and the punctuality report long before it touches PTO, so it does not consult `enablePtoTracking` and does not skip reps without an accrual rate — and reuses `workedHoursByEmpForRange_`, so “a broken day” means one thing across the codebase. THREE properties: (a) READ-ONLY — no ScriptLock, no cell, no audit row, because a diagnostic that queues behind a live punch costs more than it is worth; (b) BOUNDED AT BOTH ENDS and both ends chosen — the window ends `OPEN_PUNCH_GRACE_DAYS` before today (a rep clocked in now is open by definition, and a rep twelve hours ahead of the manager anchor looks open for most of a manager's day) and STARTS at `CONFIG.ADJUST_WINDOW_DAYS`, because past it `managerSaveDayRange` and the adjustment queue both refuse the date and the finding would name a remedy that does not exist — so the report states its own boundary and flags the days about to cross it; (c) it INVENTS NO CHANNEL — it stamps at 8am inside `runDailyChecks` and the 9am `sendAutomationHealthDigest` already emails whatever `automationProblems_` returns, and a FAILED scan stamps the failure so an empty list is never read as a clean board. The trigger is COUNT-NEUTRAL: `sendCallNotesUrgentDigest` moved out of its own standalone trigger into the new group, so the quota that bit this deployment on 2026-09-11 is untouched. | Server + Test Suite | Verify: the T pin (read-only, no mail, no PTO flag, INV-183 roster walk, ONE range build, both bounds driven incl. the expiring flag and the orphan/incomplete distinction, the failed-scan stamp, the dispatcher membership) + the editor-suite gates on `checkOpenPunches` AND `runDailyChecks` — a dispatcher's gate does not stand in for its job's.



INV-207 | **A check that reads SOURCE can only find what source LOOKS like — name resolution across the ONE global scope is a separate net, and it is DERIVED from the directory (operator 2026-09-15, `scripts/lint-server.mjs`).** Every pin in `test/client/` is built on `serverSource()` and asserts on shape; `node --check` parses each server file in isolation, where an undeclared identifier is legal. Between those two lies name resolution, and nothing checked it: `creditMonthlyPtoAccruals` passed a `perDay` that no scope declared, threw `perDay is not defined` on EVERY run for a day — the 18:00 run that was supposed to top 2026-08 up included — and the four structural pins standing over that exact function all stayed green, correctly, because the shape was perfect. The net is eslint with `no-undef` and NOTHING else, over the CONCATENATION of every `web-app/*.js` file. Three properties, each load-bearing: (a) ONE COMBINED LINT, because Apps Script loads every file into one global scope and a per-file lint would report every cross-file helper as undeclared — the noise would then be silenced by an allow-list that hides the next real one; (b) MEMBERSHIP IS THE DIRECTORY, not `filePushOrder` — clasp pushes `Tests.js` and `DevTools.js` too, so their globals need no hand-list and a new server file is covered the day it lands (INV-179: derived, not remembered); (c) the ONE hand-maintained list is the Apps Script platform's own services, which live in no file we can read — an entry there SILENCES a real bug, so it is added only against the Apps Script reference. It found three more on its first run, all pre-existing: `_TEST_OVERRIDE_KB_SS_ID` / `_FORMS_` / `_QA_` were read by their resolvers and declared nowhere, and `_withTestKb_`'s assignment worked only because Apps Script runs sloppy mode. It runs AFTER `npm ci` in CI, never in the dependency-free floor. | Server + Test Suite | Verify: the g118 wiring pin (CI step present and ordered after `npm ci`, eslint a declared devDependency, `npm run lint:server` exposed, directory enumeration, exactly one `.verify(` call) — and note that pin is ITSELF structural: the evidence the net works is the bite-check in g118 (revert the fix, the net names `20_timeclock.js:780`) plus CI running it.

INV-208 | **A number a customer is CHARGED must be server-sourced at the moment it is sent, not at the moment it was looked up (operator 2026-09-16, `oopVerifyQuotes_` / `oopQuoteLine_`).** The operator's answer that "a quoted OOP price IS a commitment — the rep processes payment on that call" is what sets the bar: a price the composer picker inserted must still be the sheet's price when `sendExternalEmail` runs, and the audit row must be able to reconstruct what the customer was told after the sheet has moved on. `oopQuoteLine_` is the ONE canonical rendering, used by the picker to build the line it inserts AND by the send to rebuild it from LIVE sheet data; **it carries the price column's LABEL (2026-09-16, OOP-C), because the operator's sheet has three customer-facing totals per item** — pick-up, with shipping, with tech delivery — and they are all correct for different fulfilments, so quoting the leftmost one to a customer whose item ships is a real shortfall on a number they pay against. `oopPriceByLabel_` resolves a quote against the column it NAMES: comparing the wrong one refuses in BOTH directions, letting an edit to the quoted column sail through while blocking an honest send over a column nobody quoted. A label the sheet no longer has returns null rather than the nearest price. A single-price sheet passes no label and reads exactly as before. the verification IS those two agreeing character for character, so the mirror is pinned behaviourally over a table rather than by inspection (a drift does not render slightly differently — it BLOCKS every OOP send with an unsatisfiable message). The fail direction is CLOSED and chosen (g41): an unreadable store, a vanished item, a blanked price, a stale price and a hand-edited line all REFUSE, each with its own message, because they are five different problems with five different fixes. A line the rep DELETED is not an error — nothing was quoted, so nothing is audited; edited and deleted are discriminated on the surviving price string. **THE BOUNDARY IS STATED, in the code and in a pin:** a rep who OVERTYPES the figure defeats this, exactly as one who types a price for an item they never picked does. The promise is that a price the PICKER inserted is server-sourced and current, not that no wrong number can reach an email — the second is unachievable from a free-text body and claiming it would be the more dangerous error. Verification runs BEFORE token creation, the PDF fetches and the send, so a refusal costs nothing and never leaves a live form link for an email that did not go out; with no quote it never reads the store at all. The quoted prices ride the EXISTING `ExternalEmailSent` row (the compliance panel already covers it, and a quote only means something attached to the send it travelled on) carrying item, exact price and effective date — and still the recipient DOMAIN only: **g36's minimization is not relaxed by the quote being commercially significant.** Client side the number never round-trips through the DOM: the rendered row carries a match INDEX, the RPC payload is parked on the results host, and the line is derived from it (g49). | Server + Client (Call Notes views) | Verify: the OOP-B mirror pin (both sides driven over a table), the `oopVerifyQuotes_` fake-sheet pin (six refusals, the silent deletion, the stated boundary), the ordering + audit-shape pin, the "never round-trips through the DOM" pin, the three OOP-B DOM pins, `test_oop_verifyQuotes_currentStaleAndDeleted` against a real sheet, and scenario S110.

INV-209 | **Area eligibility has TWO answers per item, and which restrictions LIFT is a rule, not a table (operator 2026-09-16, `oopEligibilityParse_` / `oopEligibilityForPayment_` / `oopEligibilityCheck_`).** The OOP sheet's Area Eligibility column states the rule for an order going THROUGH INSURANCE. Paying out of pocket transforms it, and the transform generalises so a value nobody has written yet still resolves: **a restriction that exists because of WHO IS PAYING lifts when nobody is billing insurance; a restriction that exists because of HOW IT PHYSICALLY GETS THERE does not.** A state limit is licensure and network — it lifts. A delivery radius is a van — it does not. `UNKNOWN NEVER LIFTS`, and that is the load-bearing line: a value the parser could not read might be a delivery constraint, and lifting it is the one guess that puts an undeliverable order in the system. Both verdicts are shown, LABELLED, rather than behind a payment-method toggle — the rep mid-call is usually deciding between the two. The grammar matches warehouse names as SUBSTRINGS against the `LocationAcceptance` registry rather than parsing English, because the operator writes prose; the registry IS the vocabulary, so a radius around a warehouse it does not know is UNKNOWN, not eligible. **That registry is a SHEET with NO seed and NO fallback (revised 2026-09-16, same day it shipped as a Script Property).** The property fell back to a CONFIG seed of bare city names, and a bare city name geocodes to the city CENTRE — so a warehouse twenty miles out of town silently made every near-boundary radius answer wrong by up to twenty miles, which is the plausible-substitute failure (g114) this very verdict exists to avoid. A missing tab now yields an EMPTY registry, every radius rule reads UNKNOWN, and the tab is named in the diagnostics AND surfaced to the rep (an unreadable registry left to produce UNKNOWNs reads like caution, not like a tab nobody created — g02). A warehouse row with no ADDRESS is dropped from the vocabulary rather than registered, because its NAME is what the grammar matches and keeping it turns an honest "we do not know that place" into a radius that can never be measured. **The same tab carries CITY rows** — places POVs/scooters are delivered to — and those are INFORMATION ONLY: they are shown beside the verdicts, outside every item row, labelled "reference only", and they never change an answer. A verdict that depended on two tables agreeing would let a stale row in one make an undeliverable item read as deliverable with nothing on screen saying which table decided; `locCityMatches_` also treats an undetermined city ('' from a geocode with no `locality`) as matching NOTHING, because a blank matching the whole list would tell a rep we deliver somewhere we have never heard of (INV-187). **Both tables and `InsurancePayors` are NAMED TABS in the KB store** — the pricing table's first reader took `getSheets()[0]`, which was correct in a spreadsheet of its own and would have read the KB tab the moment it moved, matching no `price` header and rendering every row blank rather than throwing. RADIUS is tested before STATES as defence against a plausible future relaxation of the whole-value-is-codes rule, NOT because today's code needs it — the comment says so, because an overclaiming comment gets the load-bearing thing removed. **A RADIUS `no` IS CERTAIN AND A RADIUS `yes` IS NOT** — a straight line is never longer than the drive, so a straight-line distance over the limit means the drive is too, while a `yes` near the boundary says the drive is longer rather than implying a precision the measurement does not have. UNKNOWN is never folded into NO, in the engine or in the render: "we cannot tell" and "not eligible" send a rep to two different next actions and the first is a phone call (INV-187/g114). The STATE comes from the geocoder's `administrative_area_level_1` short name, never a string-parse of `formatted_address`, which works until an address formats differently and then fails silently. The customer's address is geocoded UNCACHED and only warehouses reach the shared coordinate cache (`kbGeocodeCached_`, extracted from `kbMapDistances` so a second caller could not open a second cache with its own hygiene rules) — the privacy contract now spans three functions and its pin does too, anchored on "ONE writer, only operator-owned addresses in" rather than the old one-function statement-order argument. | Server + Client (Reference views) | Verify: the three ELIG parser/transform/verdict pins (driven against the operator's REAL column values), the endpoint shape pin, the client three-state pin, the ELIG DOM pin (both hosts, the lifting case end to end, the unplaceable warehouse), `test_oop_diagnostics_reportsRolesAndEligibilityGrouping`, `test_oop_locationAcceptance_readsBothRowKinds`, `test_oop_pricingTab_isNAMEDnotTheFirstSheet`, the `locHeaderRole_`/`locRowKind_`/`locCityMatches_` pins, the no-seed pin (which asserts the registry is BUILT EMPTY — its first three assertions all watched for the OLD shape of the defect and a bite-check showed none would have seen a warehouse hard-coded one layer down), and scenario S110. **AMENDED 2026-09-17 (Batch 2, F-15):** a geocoder SERVICE failure — quota, denial, error, throw — is its own shape (`{unavailable, status}` from `kbGeocodeOne_`; ZERO_RESULTS stays `null`) and reaches the rep as an error naming the service (`kbGeocodeUnavailableMsg_`), never as "Could not find that location" and never as a verdict; the shared coordinate cache never stores it. The OOP scenario is S112 since 2026-09-17 (it was a duplicate `S110`). Pinned by the F-15 geocoder pin.

INV-210 | **The holiday calendar is ONE list, shared with the Department Dashboard, and it REPLACES the federal list rather than adding to it (H1, 2026-09-17, `getCompanyHolidays_` / `getCdrCompanyHolidayRanges_`).** Every business-day consumer — `prevWorkdayIso_`, `metricsWorkdayIsos_`, the coverage planner, punctuality, the PTO conflict labels, `businessMinutesBetween_`, the pay statement, and the client's `mPrevWorkdayIso_` via `window.SERVER_COMPANY_HOLIDAYS` — reads `getCompanyHolidays_(year)`; `getUsHolidays_` is referenced exactly twice in the server (its definition and the one fallback) and the H1-3 pin counts them. The source is the CDR Report workbook's `Company Holidays` tab (created and maintained by `call-data-reporting`; one range per row in its Skip Dates grammar), read BY HEADER NAME with a `source` verdict (`sheet` / `empty` / `no-tab` / `unavailable`) instead of a bare `[]`; an `unavailable` read is never cached. The tab WINS the moment it holds one range; the federal list serves only on the three non-`sheet` verdicts — NEVER a union, because a merged read would keep Columbus Day a holiday after the operator listed the real closures, which is the two-calendars bug this closes, and the dashboard has no federal fallback at all, so an unlisted year is holiday-free on both sides. | Server + Client (Metrics views) | Verify: the H1-1..H1-5 pins (behavioural walks with an explicit map, the pure year-expansion, the precedence over every source shape, the reader against a fake workbook incl. the spreadsheet-tz Date cell and the cache discipline, the doGet/index.html/build.mjs wiring), `test_companyHolidays_tabWinsElseFederal`, `test_prevWorkdayIso_stepsOverHolidays`, `test_companyHolidays_readsFixtureTab`, and scenario S110.

INV-211 | **Answer % is the Department Dashboard's formula, and the standard it is judged against is READ from the dashboard's published `Dashboard Standards` tab -- no standard means NO verdict (H2, 2026-09-17, `cdrAnswerPct_` / `getCdrDashboardStandard_` / `cdrStandardShip_`).** `cdrAnswerPct_(answered, missed)` = `answered / (answered + missed)`, rounded to a WHOLE percent like the dashboard's Answer % cell (a 91.7 vs a 92 tints differently against 92), is the ONE formula and every rate site routes through it (agent, per-day, per-rep-day, the team benchmark, `teamTotals`, the ambient badge; the H2-1 pin bans a surviving `/ rung` division -- rung counts every window leg in the DQE build, so the old rate was a different number). `getCdrDashboardStandard_` reads the CDR Report's `Dashboard Standards` tab BY HEADER NAME for `CONFIG.CDR_DASHBOARD_DEPT` (Script Property override), falls back to the `*` row, reports a `source` verdict (`sheet` / `global` / `no-row` / `empty` / `no-tab` / `unavailable`), ships a NULL target on every non-sheet verdict, and never caches `unavailable`. The four metrics endpoints ship `alertThreshold` / `alertBand` / `standardSource` through `cdrStandardShip_`; `mPctClass_`, `dashPctTone_` (with the band as slack) and `getMetricsAmbient` all refuse to render a verdict without a target -- the old legacy 80/50 band and the hand-carried 85 were verdicts on numbers nobody set. **Batch 3 (2026-09-17):** a NULL band is `mtAnswerBand_`'s rule (0 = no amber tier) on BOTH surfaces (INV-217); `standardSource` is RENDERED beside the target and on the Storage Health CDR row (INV-216); no client caption carries a literal of the standard (INV-218); `cdrAnswerPct_` is NULL when answered + missed is zero (every surface dashes it); `getMetricsAmbient` judges `prevWorkdayIso_(todayMgr)`. The published Team Avg Excludes leave the BENCHMARK (`dashboardTeamAggregate_`, `metricsTeamAvgSeries_`) and never the dept totals (the dashboard's R18 ruling). Every rate-carrying cache key bumped (INV-85). | Server + Client (Metrics views, Time Clock views) | Verify: the H2-1..H2-4 pins (formula + routing, benchmark exclusions vs totals, the reader over a fake workbook incl. the scrambled-header read and the cache discipline, the ambient/tone wiring + no client mirror), the repointed #4 / PR1-2 / MW-1 / cache-key pins, `test_cdrAnswerPct_isTheDashboardFormula`, `test_teamBenchmark_subtractsPublishedExcludes`, `test_dashboardStandard_readsFixtureTab`, and scenario S111.
INV-212 | **A DQE read is bounded by a date-column SPAN, never a tail scan, and a bare Sheets serial in the Date column is a date (H3, 2026-09-17, `cdrDqeWindowSpan_` / `cdrRowDateIso_`).** Both DQE readers (`getCdrAgentMetrics_`, `getCdrDailyBreakdown_`) scan the DATE column alone, find the FIRST and LAST row inside [from, to], and read only that row span at full width -- the dashboard's R41 rule, ported. A span is correct whatever the row order is (an out-of-order backfill WIDENS it and can never fall outside it), which is why every caller KEEPS its per-row date filter: the span bounds the read, it never replaces the filter. A TAIL scan is the trap (DQE Historical Data is appended at getLastRow()+1 and only re-sorted afterwards, so a backfilled older date can sit below newer rows and a tail scan stops early and silently drops it). `cdrRowDateIso_` treats a NUMBER in the plausible range (~30000..~100000, days since 1899-12-30) as a serial and formats it in UTC -- the derived instant is UTC midnight, so the sheet's west-of-UTC tz would render the previous evening and shift the row a day (the dashboard's F-8); before H3 the row was silently dropped. | Server (Metrics) | Verify: H3-1 (serial branch, UTC formatting, no small-integer false positive), H3-2 (span behavioural: widening, null, one date-column read), H3-3 (the real `getCdrAgentMetrics_` over an out-of-order fixture equals the pre-H3 full scan value-for-value), H3-4 (both readers span + keep the filter, no full-sheet read survives), `test_metrics_cdrRowDateIso_serial`
INV-213 | **An operator-maintained tab has ONE row resolver and ONE scorer, and no reader touches a cell by POSITION (Batch 1 of the 2026-09-17 /broad-scan, F-01 + F-04).** `oopRowObj_` (header-discovered columns, `oopNameCol_` for the item) is how every OopPricing reader builds a row and `oopMatchScore_` (name OR code) is how both search surfaces score one; `oopVerifyQuotes_` keys the live sheet by the same name column the picker used, `checkOopEligibility` scores through the same scorer, and `getOopPricingDiagnostics` names an item by `o.name || o.code`. The fixture rule rides with it: a fake sheet for that tab carries the OPERATOR'S column order (code in A, item in C) and a guard forbids the item in column A — a fixture that agrees with the code's assumption cannot test it (g126). Pinned by the F-04 pin (behavioural scorer + no `rows[i][0]` / `row[0]` in any of the four readers + the verifier's `nameCol` key), the OOP-B grid guard, and the editor test `test_oop_verifyQuotes_currentStaleAndDeleted` | Subsystem: Server + Test Suite
INV-214 | **A registered `onClose` hook OWNS the close, removal included (Batch 1 of the 2026-09-17 /broad-scan, F-02).** `closeOverlay` delegates entirely to a hook registered via `ensureOverlay` (it strips `open` itself only with no hook or a throwing one — INV-145 lets a hook refuse), so every non-refusing hook removes its overlay; `ensureOverlay` recreates the node on the next open. `cnCloseSchedModal_` and `cnCloseScratchpadModal_` (which flushes a dirty save FIRST — `cnScratchSave_` reads the field synchronously) both `remove()`. Pinned by the F-02 DOM pin: both modals through `closeOverlay`, Escape and the Close button's wiring, the flush-on-close, and a sweep over every hook in `OVERLAY_CLOSE_HOOKS` present at that moment (g130) | Subsystem: Client (Call Notes views) + Client (shell)
INV-215 | **An EQUAL Clock In / Clock Out is zero hours — never a 24-hour wrap, never `null` — and the manager writers refuse it (Batch 1 of the 2026-09-17 /broad-scan, F-03).** `calcHours_` wraps on STRICT `<` (the C3 overnight decision unchanged for a genuine reversal); `managerClockOrderError_(cleanSlots)` is the one pure clock-order rule, applied by `managerSaveDay` and `managerSaveDayRange` after format validation (INV-04) and before the plan; the sheet doctor reports the pair (INV-159 amendment). The LIVE path keeps accepting a same-minute clock-out (a rep may legitimately clock straight back out) — it pays zero and is reported. Day Edit's Save is also DISABLED while the prefill is pending (F-05 — a Save in that window sent blank slots, which delete punches; g68's failed-prefill rule extended to the pending window). Pinned by the A1 equal-minute cases, the `managerClockOrderError_` pin, the smoke test `calcHours_equalMinuteIsZeroNotADay`, and the Day Edit DOM pin's pending-Save assertion | Subsystem: Server + Client (Time Clock views)
INV-216 | **A value PUBLISHED by another system and rendered here as a VERDICT carries its SOURCE to every surface that renders the verdict — a silent fallback or a silent "no standard" is indistinguishable from the real thing (19-e / Batch 3 of the cycle-20 scan, F-08 + F-09, 2026-09-17).** `getCdrDashboardStandard_` and `getCdrCompanyHolidayRanges_` both report a `source` verdict; since Batch 3 something READS each: `mStandardSourceHtml_` renders the standard's source beside the target on both Metrics heroes (muted for a missing standard, warn for an unreadable tab — g128's split), and `cdrStandardProbe_` / `cdrHolidayProbe_` ride the CDR row of `getStorageHealth` (reachable store only) so `cnStandardFindings_` / `cnHolidayFindings_` raise ONE CDR-area finding each — ok for a published row / a listed calendar, warn naming what the app does meanwhile for every other source. The holiday FALLBACK itself (federal list on empty / no-tab / unavailable) is unchanged and deferred to the operator. A new published value must ship a source AND a reader of it in the same change. | Server + Client (Metrics views, Call Notes views — Admin System) | Verify: the F-08 client pin (every source string, the warn/muted split, both heroes), the F-08/F-09 server pin (both probes through sheet / no-row / empty / unavailable / throw; the reachable-only gate), the F-08/F-09 client pin (`cnHealthFindings_` through every source; an older payload raises neither), the INV-185 fixture assertions; scenarios S110 step 5 and S111 step 5.
INV-217 | **Two surfaces that tone the same published number share ONE band rule, and a NULL band is that rule's decision, never a per-surface default (19-e / Batch 3, F-07, 2026-09-17).** `mtAnswerBand_(band)` (script_core) returns the published band or 0 — no published Amber Band means no amber tier, below the target is red — and BOTH `mPctClass_` (the Metrics team table) and `clkDashTone_`'s answered branch (the Clock KPI card) delegate to it; `dashPctTone_`'s Transfer % branch passes no band and keeps `DASH_TONE_SLACK_PP` because Transfer % has no published standard. Before this rule the table read null as 0 and the card as "not passed → 5 pt", toning one value two ways. Any new consumer of `alertBand` calls the helper. | Client (Metrics views, Time Clock views) | Verify: the F-07 pin — the two vocabularies (`m-pct-*` ↔ good/warn/crit) agree on nine (value, target, band) triples incl. null / undefined / a numeric string, the null-band and Transfer % branches, and both delegations structurally; H2-4's Clock-card regex.
INV-218 | **A client string that captions a server-published number carries NO literal of it (19-e / Batch 3, F-08, 2026-09-17).** H2 removed the hand-carried 85 everywhere except the badge tooltip's `'below ' + (res.threshold || 85) + '%'`, so a badge judged against 92 was captioned 85 — and H2-4's `alertThreshold \|\| \d` ban did not cover the word `threshold`. If the number can be absent, the caption is absent (a caption that exists only because the number does needs no fallback); the Metrics partial and the Clock partial carry no `<name> \|\| <digit>` for any standard field. | Client (Metrics views, Time Clock views) | Verify: the F-08 client pin's `threshold \|\| \d` ban plus H2-4's `alertThreshold \|\| \d|thr \|\| \d|alertBand \|\| \d` ban over both partials, and its assertion that the tooltip names the Dashboard Standards target.

### Visual Audit Stage (project-local; every `/broad-scan` MUST run it)

**A `/broad-scan` of this project is not complete until the interface has been
LOOKED AT, not only read.** This is a project-local requirement recorded here
rather than in `.claude/commands/broad-scan.md`, because that directory is
verified byte-identical to `claude-workflow-tools` and a local edit would be
silently overwritten by the next `/sync-commands`. Every audit command's first
instruction is to read CLAUDE.md, so this reaches them.

**Why it is mandatory, in the project's own numbers:** cycle 12 shipped 13
production fixes and **9 came from a visual addendum the operator had to ASK
for** — eleven prior code-lens cycles could not reach the class. Cycle 13 then
found its top four items through the same lens. The two cycles agree: the code
lens is at diminishing returns here and the interface lens is not.

Run it as **Stage 1.5**, between the broad pass and the deep dives:
1. `cd test/visual && node build.mjs && node shoot.mjs` (needs `npm ci` there
   once; Chromium is pre-installed in the web container).
2. Read `report.json` — TWO machine checks, both printed as a summary at the end
   of the run:
   - a `missing` entry means the scenario rendered a LOADER, not the real view.
     Add the fixture before trusting that screenshot.
   - **`overflowPx > 0` means the PAGE scrolls sideways** (document
     `scrollWidth − clientWidth`). Added cycle-16 Batch 4, because this section
     already demanded the measurement and nothing performed it — a squeezed
     layout and an overflowing one look IDENTICAL in a screenshot. Content
     inside a legitimate `overflow-x: auto` scroller (the tool tab bar, a wide
     data table in `.m-table-wrap`) correctly does NOT count.
3. Actually OPEN the PNGs — every scenario the run just wrote (the count lives
   in ONE place, the matrix's own summary; this step restated it twice and was
   stale twice — seams-18 F5). Compare light vs dark and wide vs compact vs
   mobile for the same scenario; that pairing is what surfaces theme and
   breakpoint defects. **Every rep-facing tool has a mobile scenario since
   cycle-16 Batch 4** — before that the matrix shot five of nine tools at ONE
   viewport, which is precisely why F2 (Reference's reader measuring 70px at
   390px) survived two interface-focused cycles. Batch 4's first run
   immediately surfaced a clipped Training heading that four sessions had
   missed. **Cycle-17 batch ⑦ closed the next three gaps:** dark parity for
   Reference / Training / Coaching, the Admin panel (light + dark — its
   `getAutomationHealth`-family fixtures now exist, with the fixture's
   top-level keys PINNED derived from the server's own return block, INV-185),
   and the first ERROR-STATE scenarios — a `?failrpc=name1,name2` query makes
   the mock invoke the FAILURE handler for those RPCs, so the
   `errorStateHtml_` paths (A12/INV-175) render on camera; a forced-fail RPC
   is NOT a missing fixture. Its first run immediately surfaced a live Low
   (the Reference LANDING pane hangs on a loader when the tree fetch fails).
   The operator-feedback round (2026-08-06) added the two redesigned status
   views — `spanish-light-wide` + `deptreq-light-wide` (fixtures cover all
   four DR tones + an overdue Spanish card) — and the 2026-08-17
   full-width round added `spanish-light-mobile` (the new `.sp-top` head+chart
   grid stacks <1024px — the breakpoint is on camera), and the 2026-08-18
   width round added `punctuality-light-wide` (the tab had never been shot —
   which is how its 780/820px caps survived two width passes), taking the
   matrix to **41** — and the fluid-pop-out-type round added
   `cn-log-light-compact-sm` (360×640, the shrunk-below-launch window the
   clamp() type exists for), taking it to **42**. **MODAL states are shootable
   since the pilot round-2 follow-ons (2026-08-24):** a scenario's optional 6th
   tuple entry (`post`) is a JS expression evaluated after nav — the sched
   modal is covered in light/dark/compact this way, and its FIRST dark shot
   immediately caught unstyled native buttons (`.cn-act-btn` is styled only
   under `.cn-card-actions`) that every light shot had made look merely plain.
   A new modal scenario is one tuple entry, not harness work — the round-3
   follow-ons used the same hook for `reference-reader-light-wide` (the OPEN
   Reference reader: article + feedback bar + comment thread), whose first
   shot led to measuring an invisible 4px edit button (`icon('pencil')`
   resolved to NO glyph — the key is `adjust`; now tripwired, see the icon-key
   pin).
   **Every block that can be EMPTY owes an empty-state scenario, and `?fixture=empty`
   is how it is shot (design handoff C7, PR 1 — 2026-09-02).** The same gap surfaced
   three times before it was a rule: `getMyCoaching` returned `{items:[]}` so the rep
   view had never been shot populated, the Admin all-clear path had never been shot
   at all, and the Needs-you empty state is the thing that changes most under its
   design. `mock.js` now parses `?fixture=empty` and, for every RPC with an entry in
   `EMPTY_FIXTURES`, returns that genuinely-empty shape instead of the populated
   fixture — ADDITIVELY, so an RPC with no entry keeps its fixture and a scenario
   never turns into a loader. A new block ships THREE scenarios: populated,
   `?fixture=empty`, and `?failrpc=<rpc>` (INV-175 — failed ≠ absent, on camera).
   The blocks that owe an entry are listed by name in the PR1-4 pin as they land
   (a fully derived "every fixture has an empty twin" scan is not expressible —
   fixtures are objects, not call sites).
   **The BROWSER timezone is a scenario dimension since 2026-09-02 (VIS-TZ).**
   A PH agent saw "Clock In" at midnight PHT; the server half was roster data,
   but the CLIENT half — any site reading the browser clock instead of the
   roster frame via `isoDateTz(empTz())` — only shows when browser and roster
   DISAGREE, which no scenario had ever arranged with the clock frozen across
   the browser's midnight. A scenario tuple's optional 7th entry
   `{ tz, utc: [h, m] }` sets Playwright's `timezoneId` (the browser) and the
   frozen instant; the mock's `?tz=<IANA>` sets the ROSTER zone the fixture rep
   carries. `clock-light-wide-pht` holds them apart (roster America/Chicago
   viewed from Asia/Manila at 16:05 UTC = 00:05 PHT = 11:05 CDT mid-shift) and
   must read entirely as the Chicago day. Its first shoot was clean. Pair any
   new browser-clock read in a rep-facing partial with a scenario in this
   dimension; the VIS-TZ pin keeps the two zones from silently collapsing
   back to equal.
   **The uncovered-tab list is DERIVED, not prose — the marker line below is
   machine-checked against the matrix (VIS-COVER), because the hand-kept
   sentence that used to sit here named 3 of 11 real gaps and a /sync-docs
   pass read it rather than checking it. Edit the marker only by adding a
   scenario or by accepting a new gap deliberately:**

   VISUAL-GAP-TABS: callNotesForms, callNotesHistory, callNotesSearch, docsManage, intakeSent, myDocs, trainingManage

   Of those, `callNotesHistory` / `callNotesSearch` are the same card
   vocabulary the Log view already shoots, and `myDocs` / `docsManage` /
   `trainingManage` are the manager/rep halves of Training & Employee Docs.
   Also uncovered and NOT tab-shaped, so the marker cannot see them: **most
   modal/overlay states** (the sched modal, the Reference reader and — since
   A4 — the Day Edit modal have `post` scenarios; the rest do not),
   and — the gap that bit on 2026-08-11 — **every ADMIN sub-tab at a mobile
   width**, which is **now CLOSED (2026-09-01)**: the Admin scenarios were
   wide-only, so `.toolbar-tabs` (a SHARED component, also the CN search field
   tabs) kept a 25px page overflow at 390px until it was measured by hand, and
   then the panes BEHIND that strip stayed uncovered for another three weeks.
   **The structural lesson is that the VIS-COVER marker works at TAB
   granularity and every Admin pane lives inside ONE tab, so five distinct
   surfaces hid behind one covered tab** — enough for a table header to ship
   stacked in a meaningless column above its own rows (`.cn-tax-head` shared
   the row's `1fr` stacking rule, so six column labels aligned with nothing and
   the first row's usage bar rode over the word "Usage"). All five panes have a
   mobile scenario now, and **VIS-ADMIN derives the pane set from the client's
   own `tab('key', 'Label')` call sites** (INV-179), so a sixth pane owes a
   scenario the day it lands. Ask the same question of any other tab that hosts
   sub-panes rather than one view. **The same shape WAS open on MANAGE
   TIME and is now closed (measured 2026-09-01 while shooting the Day Edit
   modal): the tab is covered only at WIDE, and at 390px it overflowed the page
   by 44px. Worth keeping for the misdiagnosis: the first measurement blamed
   the team-punches `.m-table`, because `getBoundingClientRect().right` on a
   table INSIDE an `overflow-x` scroller reports the table's full layout width
   and looks like an overflow. It was not — the wrap was scrolling correctly.
   The real cause was an INLINE `grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr` on the manager
   analytics pair, which beats every stylesheet rule including the shell's own
   media queries, plus a non-wrapping flex status line. **To find a real
   overflower, walk the elements whose right edge exceeds the viewport and SKIP
   any with an `overflow-x` ancestor** — a widest-rect scan cannot tell the two
   apart. An inline grid declaration is also invisible to the A2 tripwire,
   which scans stylesheets; the manager pair was the only STATIC one (the
   coverage heatmap and the training matrix compute their column counts, which
   CSS cannot express, and both sit in scrollers).**
   Every scenario also logs the pre-existing Google-Fonts
   `ERR_CONNECTION_RESET` console line (no network in the sandbox) — ignore it.
4. Re-shoot after ANY change to `styles*.html`, `styles_design_tokens.html`, or
   a view partial's CSS, and verify the fix by MEASURING the new render. V-9
   (cycle 12) and A2/FO-3 (cycle 13) were each wrong on the first reasoned
   attempt and right only after measurement.

**Split findings by what you can actually verify** — structural facts (a missing
breakpoint, a roleless control, a token that resolves wrong) are findings;
appearance judgements are OPERATOR VISUAL CHECKS written as Regression
Scenarios. Never report "this looks cramped" as a finding.

**TWO COMPANION HARNESSES live beside it, same directory, same manual posture
— both exist because the matrix's screenshots cannot answer the question they
answer.** `node print-check.mjs` measures the `@media print` block in a real
engine (`print-color-adjust` and `:has()` have no static equivalent, and the
defect it guards — a modal printing one screenful — is invisible in a PNG).
`node a11y-names.mjs` reads back the accessible name Chromium computes for
every rendered control across nine landed views AND all three public-form
templates, failing on a duplicate name; **it is the ONLY coverage
`form_public.html` has**, since that page is not built from partials and the
matrix cannot shoot it. Run `node build.mjs` first for both.

**The harness is NOT in CI** (it needs Chromium and human eyes), so nothing
enforces this except this entry.

### Policy Configuration
Policy threshold: 4/10
Consecutive cycles: 2

### Seams Audit Cadence
every 4 subsystem cycles

### Regression Scenarios
S1 | Smoke test suite | Subsystem: Test Suite
  Steps:
    - Open the Apps Script editor for the web-app project
    - In Tests.js, select `runSmokeTests` and click ▶
    - Wait for Logger output
  Expected: `Failed: 0`. Pure-logic tests run with no spreadsheet writes; integration tests show as `SKIP`. **The run opens with the `── Suite environment ──` block** (instance markers + the app's own dev/prod verdict, `ADMIN_EMAILS` classified, `MANAGER_EMAILS` + whether the test manager is listed, the fixture and save/restore properties, the store ids, the three TEST roster rows) and closes with `Expected: N registrations (S smoke · A · B)` DERIVED from the registration list — read the count there, never from a doc. A run that records fewer than expected prints `⚠ Recorded X of N`, and a name registered twice prints `⚠ Duplicate registration name(s)` by name.

S2 | Full integration test suite | Subsystem: Test Suite
  Steps:
    - Confirm the editor is pointing at a TEST copy of `CONFIG.ADP_SS_ID` (or accept that production gets `TEST_*` rows that auto-clean)
    - Select `runAllTests` and click ▶
    - Wait for Logger output
    - **(Batch S variant)** Instead run `runAllTestsPartA`, wait for it to finish, then run `runAllTestsPartB` — the split that exists for a mid-shift run, since one long execution makes every live punch queue behind the project ScriptLock
    - **(Batch S)** Read the `Expected:` line and the `── Suite environment ──` block at the top before reading any failure
    - **(Batch Q)** Confirm the `saveEmailTemplates` over-size case passes: it drives the refusal against the live store and restores what was there
  Expected: `Failed: 0`. `cleanupTestData()` removes all `TEST_*` rows at the end, resets test-employee balances to 15 annual / 10 sick, and RE-OFFBOARDS the test accounts (emails cleared) so they stay invisible to real agents between runs; the next run's setup restores the emails itself. **Part A + Part B together are exactly `runAllTests`** — each Part runs its own setup and cleanup in `finally`, and every test at a shard boundary clears its own state, so a Part stands alone; their two `Expected:` lines sum to the whole run's. The expected count is whatever the run prints (316 today). On a prod instance (`INSTANCE_IS_PROD=true`) `runAllTests` REFUSES outright and the environment block above the refusal says why — that is the runbook working, not a fault.

S3 | Employee golden path: clock in → lunch → clock out | Subsystem: Server, Client
  Steps:
    - Open the deployed web app as a non-manager employee
    - Click ClockIn, then LunchOut, then LunchIn, then ClockOut
    - Open the ADP `Timesheet` tab for today
  Expected: Each click acknowledged within ~2s. Four rows appear with correct IN/OUT direction and the punch type in COMMENTS. State chips on the page transition Working → On lunch → Working → Done.

S4 | Time-off submit, manager approve, balance deducts | Subsystem: Server, Client
  Steps:
    - As employee: submit a Full Day request for a future date
    - As manager: open Manage → Time-Off Requests → Approve
    - Refresh the employee Calendar tab and check the Employees sheet
  Expected: TimeOffRequests row Status = `Approved`; AuditLog has a `TimeOffStatusChange` row keyed to the request; employee's AnnualLeaveBalance decremented by 1.0; decision email sent (best-effort).

S5 | Adjustment beyond 7 days requires reason | Subsystem: Server
  Steps:
    - As employee, open Adjust for a date 10 days back, leave reason blank, submit
    - Re-submit with a non-empty reason
  Expected: First attempt fails with "A reason is required for adjustments more than 7 days back."; second attempt succeeds, an `ADJ-{type}` row is written, and `notifyManagerOldAdjustment_` sends a manager alert (best-effort).

S6 | Manager punch delete window | Subsystem: Server
  Steps:
    - As manager: locate a punch ≤7 days old in Recent Punches → Delete
    - Attempt to find a punch >7 days old in the widget
  Expected: Recent punch deletes (audit row + mirror clear on personal sheet); old punches don't surface as deletable in the UI. Calling `deletePunch` directly with an old date returns "Cannot delete punches older than 7 days."

S7 | Manager edit-day diff-and-apply (re-derived for the A4 break list) | Subsystem: Server, Client (Time Clock views)
  Steps:
    - As manager, open Day Edit on an ordinary single-break day → modify the Clock In time, clear Clock Out, leave the break pair untouched → Submit with a reason
    - Re-open the same day and press Save without changing anything
    - On a day with TWO breaks (see S95), open Day Edit → confirm the Breaks section lists **Break 1** and **Break 2** with their own leave/return fields and a remove button each → Save unchanged
    - Press **+ Add break**, type a third pair, and Save; re-open and remove the middle pair with its trash button, then Save
    - Remove every break row and Save
    - Type a break that returns before it leaves, one that overlaps another, and one that falls outside the clock pair — Save each
    - Open Day Edit on TODAY for a rep who is **on lunch right now** (a Lunch Out with no return yet) → the break list shows that leave with an empty return; press Save with the Clock In corrected
    - Type a return with no leave, and a half pair with another pair after it — Save each
    - Type into one break row, then press **+ Add break** before saving
    - Fill the **To** date on a day that has two break rows
    - **(Batch 1, 2026-09-17)** Open Day Edit and press Save IMMEDIATELY, before the punches paint
    - **(Batch 1, 2026-09-17)** Type the same time into Clock In and Clock Out and Save
  Expected: the clock half is unchanged from before A4 — one update, one delete, one no-op, zero adds; one `ADJ-{type}`-tagged audit row per change carrying the manager's email and the reason. Re-saving an untouched day writes NOTHING (the no-op compare is on HH:mm, not HH:mm:ss, so a live punch's real seconds don't read as a change). **The submitted break list IS the day:** the two-break day survives a no-op save (before A4 it was silently collapsed to one pair), adding leaves N+1, removing the middle pair deletes exactly that pair's rows and not the last one, and an emptied list deletes every break row while the clock punches stay. **A rep who is out RIGHT NOW saves normally:** a TRAILING leave with no return is an IN-PROGRESS break, not damage — the punch clock creates that state every day at lunch — so it round-trips as a lone Lunch Out and the clock correction lands, with no churn on the untouched leave. Refusing it (the first A4 build did) made Day Edit unsavable for anyone on lunch; `test_managerSaveDay_mixedChanges` caught it, having encoded the pre-A4 behaviour correctly. Every OTHER half IS refused by name — a RETURN with no leave is not producible by the punch flow, and a half followed by more rows means those rows are mis-entered — as are "returns at or before it leaves", "Breaks overlap" and "falls outside the shift"; nothing is written (check the sheet). Pressing Add preserves what was already typed in the other rows. With **To** filled, the Add control is disabled and the modal states that range mode applies one pair per day; submitting more than one pair anyway is refused by the server naming the same reason. **(Batch 1, 2026-09-17)** Save reads "Loading…" and is DISABLED until the punches land — a Save in that window used to send blank slots, which delete punches (g68's pending window, INV-215). An EQUAL Clock In / Clock Out is refused by name (`managerClockOrderError_`, both writers) and nothing is written; a reversed pair is still the C3 overnight wrap.
S8 | ADP-format export (manual range) | Subsystem: Server
  Steps:
    - As manager: Manage → Export → set date range covering recent activity → run
  Expected: A new Sheet is created in Drive, rows sorted by date / empId / time, COMMENTS column cleared, two-row header copied. AuditLog has an `AdpExport` row recording the row count and file ID.

S9 | Automated daily missed-punch alert | Subsystem: Server
  Steps:
    - Insert a TEST_ ClockIn punch dated yesterday with no matching ClockOut
    - In the Apps Script editor, run `sendDailyMissedPunchAlerts`
    - Run `cleanupTestData()` afterwards
  Expected: Logger shows one missed entry; one employee-targeted email and one manager-targeted summary email are queued via MailApp; no exception thrown if `MANAGER_EMAILS` is empty.

S10 | Cross-timezone live status on dashboard | Subsystem: Server
  Steps:

    - Inspect employee rows whose `Timezone` is `Asia/Kolkata` or `Asia/Manila`
  Expected: Each row shows last-punch time in BOTH the employee's tz abbreviation (IST / PHT) and the manager's (CST). Conversion matches `convertDateTime_` (e.g. 14:30 IST May 17 → 04:00 CST May 17).

S11 | Min-interval debounce on live punches | Subsystem: Server, Client
  Steps:
    - As employee, click ClockIn, then immediately click LunchOut within ~5s
    - Wait until 30s have elapsed since the ClockIn and retry
  Expected: Second click within 30s returns `Your last punch was just Xs ago. Please wait Ys before punching again (if you made a mistake, use Adjust instead).` and writes no row. After the window elapses, the second punch succeeds. Adjustments via the Adjust modal are NOT blocked by this rule.

S12 | Self-undo within 5 minutes | Subsystem: Server, Client
  Steps:
    - As employee, click ClockIn now
    - Click the Undo button on the just-recorded punch within 5 minutes
    - Check the ADP Timesheet sheet and AuditLog
    - Wait > 5 minutes (or set system clock forward) and try to undo a fresh punch
    - Try to undo an adjustment punch via the same UI (manual call to `selfDeletePunch` if needed)
  Expected: Within window — punch row removed; AuditLog has a `PunchSelfUndo` row (not `PunchDelete`); personal-sheet mirror cleared. After window — "Self-undo only works within 5 minutes…" error. Adjustment punch — "Cannot self-undo an adjustment. Use Adjust again to fix it." error.

S13 | Manager files time-off (with auto-approve) | Subsystem: Server, Client
  Steps:
    - As manager, in the Manage tab open the "File time-off on behalf of employee" modal
    - Pick an employee, type=Full Day, future date, check Auto-approve, submit
    - Check TimeOffRequests sheet, AuditLog, and the employee's AnnualLeaveBalance
    - Repeat without Auto-approve and confirm Pending behavior
  Expected: With auto-approve — row written with Status=`Approved` directly (no Pending stage); balance decremented by 1.0; AuditLog row notes `filed by manager, auto-approved`; employee gets the decision email. Without auto-approve — Status=`Pending`, no balance change yet, no email; an `updateTimeOffStatus` to Approved later behaves identically to S4.

S14 | Teammate status low-privilege view (+ the note-10 presence chip) | Subsystem: Server, Client
  Steps:
    - As a non-manager employee, open the Clock page
    - Observe the teammate status card below Actions
    - Inspect the network response for `getTeammateStatus` in DevTools
    - **(note 10, 2026-09-10)** From a SECOND account that has NOT clocked in today, open the app and click anywhere; watch the Network tab for `recordPresence`; click and type for a few more minutes; then reload the first account's Dashboard and read that rep's card
    - From the second account, clock in and reload the first Dashboard again; then clock out from the second account and reload once more
    - Leave the second account's window open untouched for 35+ minutes (or clear the `presence_v1:<id>` CacheService entry from the editor) and reload the first Dashboard
    - Read your OWN card on the first account after clicking around without clocking in
  Expected: Card shows teammate names + their current status (clocked_in / on_lunch / not_in / clocked_out). The response payload contains ONLY `{ name, status, isSelf, activeNotIn }` per teammate — no email, no employee ID, no last-punch time, no timezone field, and NO presence timestamp (INV-24). If `CONFIG.SHOW_TEAMMATE_STATUS = false`, response is `{ enabled: false, teammates: [] }`. **The presence chip:** the second account's FIRST click sends exactly one `recordPresence`; further clicks/keys inside ten minutes send NOTHING (one send per 10 min per window — an idle or left-open window costs nothing). The first account's card then shows that rep with a warn-toned **active · not clocked in** chip on the status line, the card summary reads "… away · 1 active but not in", and the chip's tooltip states that shift hours and time off are not checked. After the rep clocks in the chip is GONE (a working state is never flagged); after they clock out it reads **active · clocked out**. With the stamp expired (no gesture for ~30 min) the chip is gone again. Your OWN card never carries the chip, whatever you do (self is never flagged). A CacheService failure renders NO chips anywhere rather than an error card — a missed chip is the safe failure.

S15 | Per-employee PTO toggle | Subsystem: Server, Client
  Steps:
    - In the Employees sheet, set column K (`PtoEnabled`) to `FALSE` for a contractor row
    - As that employee, reload the web app and open the Calendar tab
    - Submit a time-off request and have a manager approve it
    - Check the decision email body
  Expected: Calendar tab hides the PTO ring + balance line entirely; the leave-balance UI does not render. Time-off submit/approve still works (the TimeOffRequest row is created, AuditLog written), but no balance is deducted because the contractor's `ptoEnabled` short-circuits both `getEmployeeState` and the decision email's balance line. Setting back to blank or `TRUE` restores the full UI.

  Expected: Both views still render `09:00:00` / `9:00 AM` correctly because `normalizeTime_` reformats the coerced Date back through the spreadsheet's timezone. A regression here surfaces as `Sat Dec 30 1899 …` strings in the UI.

Manual copy re-renders the same string.

S19 | Call Notes — email composer with preview gate | Subsystem: Client (Call Notes), Server
  Steps:
    - As an enrolled rep with at least one note today, click the envelope on a card
    - Select one or more departments, type "Verified Shipping" into Update Type, fill the shipping subform, click Preview →
    - Confirm the Preview button shows the in-button loader ("Saving note…" when Note Reference edits are pending, then "Building preview…") instead of sitting inert
    - Edit a Note Reference field in the modal (e.g. fill a blank Transferred To) before previewing — confirm the edit lands on the note card too after Preview (the composer saves through `updateCallNote` first)
    - Blank the Patient Name & TRX field and click Preview → a dismissible "No Patient Name & TRX" confirm appears (Continue anyway / Go back) — Go back returns to the form with the field there to fill; Continue proceeds (operator 2026-08-27 — never a hard block)
    - Confirm the preview reflects departments + subject + rendered HTML body
    - Click Send Email
    - Inspect Mail (or test mailbox), the note row's EmailedAt + EmailDepartments columns, and AuditLog
    - **(Batch 2, 2026-09-17)** Once: fail the department-config fetch (network off on the first Log enter), dismiss the toast, restore the network and open the composer
  Expected: Preview returns rendered HTML matching the warm-paper aesthetic (no CSS variables — inline hex), correct To/CC/Subject; the From display name is the AGENT'S NAME ALONE (no org suffix — the 2026-08-27 correction) with Reply-To the agent, and the sending agent receives a self-BCC copy. After Send, the card shows the envelope as filled (is-sent); EmailedAt is populated; AuditLog has a `CallNoteEmail` row. Closing the modal mid-flow at the form step or the preview step does NOT send (uncommitted Note Reference edits toast "Note edits discarded"). **(Batch 2, 2026-09-17)** A failed department-config fetch is NOT cached — the next composer open re-asks and the Recipients grid fills (it used to stay empty, Preview refusing, for the whole session; g129).
S20 | Call Notes — flag trio + resolved state | Subsystem: Server, Client (Call Notes)

  Expected: Each toggle updates the FlagType column and writes a `CallNoteFlag` audit row. Flag types are mutually exclusive — switching from action to training also clears the Resolved column. Stale-flag pulse renders only on action-flag + unresolved + past STALE_FLAG_HOURS. `setCallNoteResolved` rejects non-action flags with "Only action-flagged notes can be resolved."

S22 | Call Notes — EOD digest fires for stale action flags | Subsystem: Server

    - From the Apps Script editor, run `sendCallNotesEodDigest` while the rep's local time is within their EOD hour (`EOD_WARNING_HOUR`); in production an hourly trigger fires this automatically
  Expected: Logger shows the rep was emailed (their local hour equals the EOD hour) and unresolved-action count > 0. The email body has the warm-paper aesthetic + lists the unresolved note. A rep whose local hour isn't the EOD hour, or with no enrolled Sheet or no unresolved action flags, is silently skipped.

    - Type a 1-character query → no search fires (minimum 2)

S24 | Call Notes — manager training-queue + review-candidate digests | Subsystem: Server
  Steps:
    - Have several reps file training-flagged + review-flagged notes across the past week
    - From the Apps Script editor, run `sendCallNotesWeeklyDigests`
    - Inspect the manager mailbox
  Expected: Two separate emails arrive (Training Queue, Review Candidates), each listing notes from the past 7 days with rep name + caller + issue + resolution — the Review digest additionally carries a `Comment:` line (html + text bodies, esc_'d) for review-flagged notes that have a `subformData.reviewComment` (pilot round 1). A CLEAN-AND-EMPTY queue is silently skipped (no email) — but since cycle-17 batch ② a queue whose walk skipped unreadable rep Sheets SENDS, with a "N rep Sheet(s) could not be read … may be incomplete" warning line (a failed read is not an empty queue, INV-187); an empty-but-unreadable queue sends a warning-only digest. The function never throws.

S25 | Compact mode + per-tool pop-out (cross-tool) | Subsystem: Client (shell)
  Steps:
    - From Call Notes, click the pop-out icon → confirm a 480×800 chromeless window opens (sidebar + header collapsed)
    - Switch the main window to Time Clock, click pop-out again → confirm a SECOND window opens (the Call Notes pop-out stays open — both coexist)
    - From the main window on Call Notes, click pop-out again → confirm it FOCUSES the existing Call Notes pop-out (no duplicate); same for Time Clock
    - Resize each pop-out, close + reopen each → confirm each restores its OWN size/position
    - In a pop-out, navigate between views (Call Notes ↔ Time Clock ↔ Manage) and resize → confirm the geometry stays under the tool the window was opened for
  Expected: Window name is `umsTeamToolsCompact_<tool>` and geometry key `umsPopoutGeom_<tool>`, so one window per tool — Call Notes + Time Clock pop-outs coexist; a repeat click on a tool focuses that tool's window. A legacy `umsPopoutGeom` seeds size only. **The Call Notes pop-out additionally SELF-SIZES once on launch** (`cnPopoutFitToTemplate_`, operator feedback 2026-08-06): after the Log view renders, the window resizes so the whole note template (`.cnv-layout`) is visible — verify the save card isn't cut off on first open, on any display scaling; a later manual resize still persists and is restored. All tool views render without horizontal overflow; the compact Time Clock hides the Needs-you block (and makes no `getMyPendingTasks` call) + greeting kicker and tightens paddings (the world-clock strip it used to hide is retired — PR 6); action grid (Time Clock) and dept-chip grid (Call Notes) stack 2-col → 1-col gracefully.

The Coach button renders STYLED — info-toned (`.cn-mgr-coach-btn`) beside the destructive Delete, in a wrapping actions row — not as a bare native button (it had shipped with no stylesheet rule); the Per-Rep view is on camera as `cn-teamnotes-rep-light-wide`.

Clearing or typing or switching field tab all drop exact mode and re-fire as a substring search with the same query.

AuditLog has a `CallNotesExport` row with note count + new fileId. Empty range returns a friendly error. Non-manager call returns "Manager access required."

S30 | Trigger handlers reject non-manager callers via google.script.run | Subsystem: Server
  Steps:

      and the same for `runDailyExportCheck`, `sendCallNotesEodDigest`, `sendCallNotesWeeklyDigests`, `sendCallNotesUrgentDigest`, `purgeExpiredFormData`
    - Repeat as a manager, expecting them to run normally (or no-op for empty queues / disabled retention)
  Expected: Each non-manager call throws "manager access required" via `assertManagerCaller_`. No emails are sent, no Sheets are created, no roster work is done. Manager calls proceed as the time-based triggers do.

S31 | Optimistic submit + failure revert | Subsystem: Client (Call Notes views), Server
  Steps:
    - Open Chrome DevTools → Network → throttle to "Slow 3G"
    - As an enrolled rep, fill out a note and press Ctrl/⌘+Shift+C
    - Observe the rolling stack DURING the in-flight request
    - Wait for the server response
    - Repeat with the spreadsheet ID temporarily wrong (force an RPC failure)
  Expected: The new card appears in the stack IMMEDIATELY with reduced opacity + a "Saving" badge in place of action buttons. Form is cleared at the same time; clipboard already holds the formatted note (paste works before the network returns). Once the server confirms, the card swaps to its confirmed state with full action buttons. On forced failure, the pending card disappears and a "Save failed" toast states the recovery outcome: the snapshot is restored into the form ONLY if the form is still empty; if the rep has started typing the next note the form is left untouched (the failed note stays on the clipboard); if the rep navigated away the snapshot is parked in the sticky-draft slot and restores on the next Log view enter. AuditLog has `CallNoteCreate` only on success.

  Expected: A "Draft restored" toast appears on Log view enter and the form is pre-populated with the prior values, the prior flag selection, and the training question (if training was selected). The completion timer continues from the original start time (so a refresh doesn't reset the clock). Pressing Clear Note clears the draft from localStorage; the next view enter starts fresh.

S36 | Personal pin — toggle, 3-cap, tray, dedupe | Subsystem: Server, Client (Call Notes views)

    - Try to pin a 4th note

    - Unpin one; pin a different one

  Expected: Each pin triggers an optimistic tray update (card moves up immediately, before server response); on failure the tray reverts. The 4th pin returns "You already have 3 pinned notes (the max). Unpin one before pinning another." Pinned-from-today does NOT also appear in the rolling stack (cnRenderStack_ dedupes by noteId). The pinned tray spans all dates so a pin from another day stays visible. Deleting a pinned note removes it from both the tray cache and the audit log shows `CallNoteDelete` (not `CallNotePin`).

S37 | End-of-shift Stats tab with median completion time | Subsystem: Server, Client (Call Notes views)
  Steps:
    - As a manager, open Call Notes → Team Notes → Stats tab
    - Confirm the default date is today (in manager tz)
    - Inspect the per-rep cards
    - Pick a prior date that had activity
    - Submit a note as a rep (with optimistic UI tracking completion time), then re-open Stats for today
    - **(cycle-16 F1)** Temporarily blank one rep's Employees column L, or point it at an id the deployer cannot open, then re-open the Stats tab for a date that rep worked
  Expected: One card per enrolled rep. Each shows total notes, flag breakdown (action/training/review), resolved count, emails sent, median note completion time, shift span. Median is in `Xm Ys` format; outliers > 30 min are excluded from the median (stored as null upstream). Reps with no `completionSeconds` data (notes filed before the timer was instrumented) show "no data yet" for median. Stats refresh on date-picker change. **With an unreadable Sheet the rep is still LISTED (their CDR row is real) but every note-derived column — Notes / Action / Training / Review / Emails / Median — renders a warn-toned em dash with a "notes Sheet unreadable" tooltip, and Coverage renders an em dash, NOT `0` and NOT a CRIT-toned 0% badge.** Sorting by any of those columns groups the unavailable reps with the other unknowns rather than interleaving them with reps who genuinely logged zero. A rep with column L simply BLANK is not enrolled and does not appear at all (INV-35) — that is a different state from a failed read.

With the flag off (default), no mic icons render anywhere. Reps on non-Chrome browsers (Firefox) never see the mic because `webkitSpeechRecognition` isn't defined.

S41 | Metrics — My Stats self-view with sparkline | Subsystem: Server, Client (Metrics views)
  Steps:
    - As an enrolled rep, open Metrics → My Stats
    - Confirm the default is the **Yesterday** preset landing on the previous WORKDAY (on a Monday it shows Friday — operator 2026-08-17; CDR data is never same-day) and the hero label reads 'Yesterday'
    - Click **Custom…** → the Day + Range inputs reveal; change the Day to a prior working day with known CDR activity
    - Click the 7D preset → confirm the aggregate hero AND that the "Trends · you vs team avg" section STAYS rendered (headed "trailing 30 days ending <previous workday>") plus Best/Worst day chips under the delta
    - Inspect the hero sparkline (dashed avg baseline + dashed warn-toned target line) and the delta line's "· target 85%"
    - Check the rail's **Transfers** row (count + % of calls + sparkline in Today mode)
    - If coverage < 80% today, click "File them in Call Notes →" in the hint
  Expected: Hero shows % Answered + delta vs the period daily average + target; rail shows Notes Filed / Answered / Missed / Avg Talk / Transfers / Total Talk. The Custom row is HIDDEN while a preset is active (the [hidden] gotcha) and auto-opens when the selection matches no preset. The trend section renders in EVERY mode (#1 — in range mode it is filled from the Today payload, best-effort). The coverage CTA appears only for TODAY in single-day mode and lands on the Call Notes Log with a toast (the CLK_NAV_HINT mechanism). If CDR_SS_ID is not configured, shows a friendly "No call data found" message + notes count (an unreadable notes Sheet shows an em dash, never 0 — C17-14).

S42 | Metrics — Team Metrics date-range + presets | Subsystem: Server, Client (Metrics views)
  Steps:

    - Click the "7D" preset chip; confirm it shows PRESSED and the hidden From/To (behind **Custom…**) update; the table re-fetches with aggregated data
    - Click "30D"; confirm the table includes more data AND the hero shows a per-day sparkline over the SELECTED range with "vs period daily average" (#8)

  Expected: Preset chips carry aria-pressed state; a manual range un-presses them (Custom shows pressed). Per-rep table shows Rung, Answered, Missed, % Answered (green band starts at the shipped 85 target — a rep at 80–84.9% is AMBER), ATT, Notes, Coverage, Transfers; the rail includes a Transfers row when the Transfer read succeeded. Multi-day ranges SHOW the range sparkline (single-day keeps the 30-day trend + "vs 30-day team average"). From > To auto-corrects. The copied TSV follows the current sort, has plain values only, and exports an unreadable notes Sheet as blank (never 0). The two info-tone reference lists are folded behind the diagnostics disclosure; the likely-name-mismatch warning stays visible above it. A non-manager opening Team Metrics (the tab is visible to everyone since 2026-08-18) sees the team hero + rail + trend with a "per-rep breakdown is visible to managers" note — and a direct `getTeamMetrics` call returns NO `reps[]`/diagnostics fields (`repView:true`).
